------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial reasoning rules apply to this contradiction. 
The rule "If XXX is inside YYY and YYY is covered by ZZZ then XXX is inside ZZZ" could potentially apply if we consider the crime scene (XXX) and its possible locations (YYY and ZZZ as different rooms or areas).

**Step 3:** If applicable rules exist, explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
However, upon closer inspection, the direct application of the given rules to the contradiction between the testimonies and evidences regarding the crime scene's location is not straightforward. The contradiction arises more from the implication of blood found in multiple locations (elevator, Committee chamber) versus the testimony limiting the crime scene to the P.I.C. meeting room.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed rules apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
The spatial reasoning here involves understanding that bloodstains in different locations (as indicated by Evidence 19 and Evidence 21) imply movement or presence of the victim or culprit in those areas, contradicting the testimony that the crime scene is exclusively the P.I.C. meeting room. This suggests a more complex scenario than what the testimony describes.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format.

**Step 3:** Since none of the listed rules directly apply to the contradiction between the bloodstains on the gloves (Evidence 3) and the testimonies about blood on both hands of the assailant (Testimonies 0 and 2), we proceed to use general spatial reasoning to identify the contradiction.

**Step 4:** Using general spatial reasoning, the contradiction arises because if the assailant had blood on both hands as testified, we would expect to find bloodstains on both gloves if the assailant was wearing them. The presence of a bloodstain only on the right glove (Evidence 3) contradicts the testimonies that imply blood should be visible on both hands (and by extension, both gloves if they were worn during the crime).

**Step 5:** Given the analysis, the contradiction is between Evidence 3 (Defendant's Leather Gloves) and Testimony 0 (by Fairplay) or Testimony 2 (by Furst), as both testimonies imply blood on both hands, contradicting the evidence of a bloodstain on only one glove.
